# String of Pearls — chapter 6: paired reading

<!-- pair: MTP-000321; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༄༦

[Electronic chapter marker 6.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-001][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-002]

<!-- pair: MTP-000322; format: prose; role: main_text -->
རྡོ་རྗེ་འཛིན་པས་ཡང་གསོལ་པ

The holder of the vajra again petitioned:[^T06-001][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-003]

<!-- pair: MTP-000323; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཀྱེ་ཀྱེ་རྡོ་རྗེ་འཆང་ཆེན་གསང་སྔགས་བདག
སངས་རྒྱས་ཐམས་ཅད་གཅིག་ཉིད་པའི
དེ་ཉིད་དགོངས་པ་ཇི་ལྟར་ལགས

“O, O great Vajradhara, Lord of Secret Mantra!
The oneness of all buddhas—
what is that very enlightened intent like?[^T06-001][^T06-002][^CH06-G001056][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-003][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-004][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-005][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-006]

<!-- pair: MTP-000324; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཉོན་མོངས་ལྔ་ཡི་དྲི་མ་ཡིས
སེམས་ཅན་ཇི་ལྟར་བཅིངས་པར་འགྱུར
བཅོམ་ལྡན་འདས་ཀྱིས་བདག་ལ་གསུངས

Through the stains of the five afflictions,
how do sentient beings become bound?
Bhagavān, speak to me.”[^T06-001][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-003][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-007][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-008]

<!-- pair: MTP-000325; format: prose; role: main_text -->
བཅོམ་ལྡན་འདས་ཀྱིས་བཀའ་སྩལ་པ

The Bhagavān spoke:[^T06-001][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-003][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-009]

<!-- pair: MTP-000326; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སངས་རྒྱས་ཀུན་གྱི་བདེན་གཅིག་ནི
རང་བཞིན་ནི་ངོ་བོ་ཡིས
དེ་ཉིད་ལས་ནི་ཁྱད་པར་ཕྱེ

“As for the single truth of all buddhas,
intrinsic nature, through essence,
is distinguished from that itself.[^T06-003][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-003][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-010][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-011][^T06-L001][^T06-L002]

<!-- pair: MTP-000327; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ངོ་བོ་ཡེ་ཤེས་སྣང་བ་ལ
རང་བཞིན་ཀུན་ཁྱབ་སྐུ་ལྔར་ལྡན
འབྲས་བུ་ཐིག་ལེ་ཉག་གཅིག་གོ

In the appearance of essence, primordial knowing,
intrinsic nature pervades all, endowed with five embodiments.
The result is the unique sphere.[^T06-003][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-003][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-012][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-013][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-014][^T06-L002]

<!-- pair: MTP-000328; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སྣང་བ་ཉིད་ལ་ཟུང་འཇུག་སྟེ

In appearance itself there is union;[^T06-004][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-003][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-015][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-016][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-017]

<!-- pair: MTP-000329; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༨༡

[Printed page 481.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-018][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-019]

<!-- pair: MTP-000330; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སྟོང་པ་ལ་ནི་འོད་གསལ་ལོ

in emptiness there is clear light.[^T06-004][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-016][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-020]

<!-- pair: MTP-000331; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ངོ་བོ་ཉིད་ནི་ཅིག་ཆར་ཏེ
རང་བཞིན་ཉིད་ལ་རིམ་གྱིས་སོ

Essence itself is simultaneous;
within intrinsic nature itself, [it is] gradual.[^T06-004][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-016][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-021][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-022]

<!-- pair: MTP-000332; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཆོས་ཉིད་སྟོང་པ་ཀུན་ཁྱབ་ལ
མཐའ་ཡི་རྡུལ་དང་བྲལ་བ་ཉིད
རང་བཞིན་སྣང་བ་འོད་གསལ་ལ
འཁྲུལ་པའི་དྲི་མ་བྲལ་བ་ཉིད

In the all-pervading, empty nature of phenomena,
there is freedom from the dust of extremes.
In the appearance of intrinsic nature, clear light,
there is freedom from the stain of delusion.[^T06-005][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-016][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-023][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-024][^T06-L001][^T06-L002]

<!-- pair: MTP-000333; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཆོས་སྐུ་འོད་གསལ་བདེ་ཆེན་ལ
དངོས་པོའི་མཐའ་རྣམས་སྟོང་པ་སྟེ
རང་བྱུང་རིག་པའི་རྒྱལ་པོ་ལ
མཐོ་དང་དམའ་བའི་ཡུལ་ལས་འདས

In dharma embodiment, clear light, great bliss,
the extremes of entities are empty.
In the naturally arising king of awareness,
[it is] beyond the domains of high and low.[^T06-005][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-016][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-025][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-026][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-027]

<!-- pair: MTP-000334; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ང་ཉིད་སངས་རྒྱས་དགོངས་པ་ལས
འཁོར་བ་ང་ཡིན་མྱང་འདས་ང
ཆོས་ཀྱང་ང་ཡིན་ཆོས་མིན་ང

I myself, from the enlightened intent of buddhas,
am saṃsāra; I am nirvāṇa.
I am phenomena; I am non-phenomena.[^T06-006][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-016][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-028][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-029][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-030][^T06-L001]

<!-- pair: MTP-000335; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དམྱལ་བ་ལ་སོགས་ངན་སོང་ང
དཀྱིལ་འཁོར་ལྷ་དང་སྔགས་ཀྱང་ང
སངས་རྒྱས་སོགས་དཀོན་མཆོག་ང

I am the lower destinies, hells and the like.
I am mandalas, deities, and mantra.
I am the precious ones, buddhas and the like.[^T06-006][^CH06-G001085][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-016][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-031][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-032][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-033][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-034][^T06-L001]

<!-- pair: MTP-000336; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༨༢

[Printed page 482.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-035][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-036]

<!-- pair: MTP-000337; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སྟོན་པ་འཁོར་དང་བསྟན་པ་ང
རང་གི་འཁོར་སྡུད་སྡུད་པ་ང
ལྟ་དང་སྒོམ་དང་སྤྱོད་པ་ང
དབང་དང་དམ་ཚིག་སྡོམ་པ་ང

I am the teacher, retinue, and teaching.
I am gathering—gathering my own retinue.
I am view, cultivation, and activity.
I am empowerment, commitments, and vows.[^T06-007][^CH06-G001089][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-033][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-037][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-038][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-039][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-040][^T06-L005]

<!-- pair: MTP-000338; format: verse; role: main_text -->
མཆོད་པ་དང་བསམ་གཏན་ཏིང་འཛིན་ང
ཁྲུས་དང་གཙང་སྦྲ་དྲི་མེད་ང
དགེ་དང་མི་དགེ་སྤྱོད་པ་ང
མདོ་སྡེ་འདུལ་བ་མངོན་པ་ང

I am offerings, meditative stability, and deep absorption.
I am washing, cleanliness, and stainlessness.
I am virtuous and nonvirtuous activity.
I am sūtra discourses, discipline, and higher doctrine.[^T06-008][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-033][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-041][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-042][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-043]

<!-- pair: MTP-000339; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སྟོང་དང་མི་སྟོང་བདེན་གཉིས་ང
སྤྱི་དང་ཐེག་པའི་ཁྱད་པར་ང
སྡིག་དང་ལས་དང་རྣམ་སྨིན་ང
འཇིག་རྟེན་སྣོད་དང་བཅུད་ཀྱང་ང

I am the two truths, empty and not empty.
I am the general and the distinctions of the vehicles.
I am wrongdoing, action, and ripening.
I am the world, its receptacle and contents.[^T06-009][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-033][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-044][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-045]

<!-- pair: MTP-000340; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དེ་ཕྱིར་འགྲོ་དང་མི་འགྲོ་ང
ལས་དང་དེ་ཉིད་ཉོན་མོངས་དྲི་མ་ང
དབང་པོ་དང་ནི་ཡུལ་རྣམས་ང

Therefore I am what goes and what does not go.
I am action and that itself, the stain of affliction.
I am the faculties and objects.[^T06-009][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-033][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-046][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-047][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-048][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-049][^T06-L001]

<!-- pair: MTP-000341; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༨༣

[Printed page 483.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-050][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-051]

<!-- pair: MTP-000342; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཕུང་པོ་ཁམས་དང་སྐྱེ་མཆེད་ང
ཤ་དང་ཁྲག་དང་རུས་པ་ང
མིང་དང་ཡི་གེ་སྒྲ་གཟུགས་ང
གཟུང་འཛིན་ཡུལ་དང་བདག་རྟོག་ང

I am the aggregates, elements, and sense bases.
I am flesh, blood, and bone.
I am names, letters, words, and forms.
I am apprehended object and apprehending subject, objects, and conceptualizing a self.[^T06-010][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-048][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-052][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-053][^T06-L004]

<!-- pair: MTP-000343; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སྡུག་བསྔལ་མ་རིག་འཁྲུལ་པ་ང
སྒྲ་དང་དྲི་དང་རོ་རེག་ང
ཚ་དང་གྲང་བའི་ལོག་འཛིན་ང
བཀྲེས་དང་སྐོམ་དང་རྨོངས་པ་ང
བདེ་དང་སྡུག་བསྔལ་བཏང་སྙོམས་ང

I am suffering, ignorance, and delusion.
I am sound, smell, taste, and touch.
I am the mistaken apprehension of heat and cold.
I am hunger, thirst, and bewilderment.
I am pleasure, suffering, and equanimity.[^T06-011][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-048][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-054][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-055][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-056][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-057][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-058][^T06-L005]

<!-- pair: MTP-000344; format: verse; role: main_text -->
མདོར་ན་ཇི་ལྟར་སྣང་བ་ང
ང་ནི་མི་འཕམ་ཀུན་ལས་རྒྱལ་བའོ
ང་ནི་བཅོས་པའི་ཚིག་མེད་ཀུན་ལས་ཕགས་པ་ཡིན
ང་ནི་རྟག་ཆད་ཉིས་མེད་ཀུན་ལྟར་འཇུག་པ་ཡིན

In brief, I am however [things] appear.
I am undefeated, victorious over all.
I have no contrived utterance; I am exalted beyond all.
I have neither permanence nor annihilation; I enter in every manner.[^T06-012][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-048][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-059][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-060][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-061][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-062][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-063][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-064][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-065][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-066]

<!-- pair: MTP-000345; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༨༤

[Printed page 484.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-067][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-068]

<!-- pair: MTP-000346; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ང་ནི་མཐོང་བའི་ཡུལ་མེད་ཀུན་ལ་མངོན་དུ་སྣང
ང་ནི་ཐམས་ཅད་གཏིང་རྫོགས་ཀུན་ལས་ཆེ་བ་ཡིན
ང་ནི་བགྲོད་པའི་ལམ་མེད་ཀུན་ལས་ཕ་རོལ་ཕྱིན
ང་ནི་གཅིག་པུ་ཉིད་དེ་ཀུན་ལ་འབྲེལ་པ་ཡིན

I have no object of seeing; I appear manifestly to all.
I am complete to the depths of everything; I am greater than all.
I have no path to traverse; I have passed beyond all.
I am unique; I am related to all.[^T06-013][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-065][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-069][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-070][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-071][^T06-L002][^T06-L004]

<!-- pair: MTP-000347; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ང་ནི་གནས་པའི་ས་མེད་ཀུན་ལ་བརྟེན་པ་ཡིན
ང་ནི་འགྲན་པའི་ཟླ་མེད་ཀུན་ལ་ཉག་གཅིག་གོ
ང་ནི་བེམ་རིག་གཉིས་མེད་ཀུན་ལ་ཁྱབ་པ་ཡིན
ང་ནི་མུན་པའི་གོས་གྱོན་ཀུན་ལ་སྣང་བ་སྟོན

I have no ground on which to abide; I depend on all.
I have no rival to compete with; I am unique among all.
For me, insentient and aware are not two; I pervade all.
I wear the clothing of darkness; I show appearance to all.[^T06-013][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-065][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-072][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-073][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-074][^T06-L002]

<!-- pair: MTP-000348; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ང་ཡིས་རི་བོ་རྒྱ་ཐེག་བྱས་པས་སྟོབས་ཀྱང་ཆེ
ང་ཡིས་རྒྱ་མཚོ་ཧུབ་ཀྱིས་སྟོབ་པས་ལྒོབ་ཆེ
ང་ཡིས་རི་བོ་ཏིལ་འབྲུར་འཛུད་པས་མཛད་པ་ཆེ

Having made a རྒྱ་ཐེག of mountains, my strength is great.
Through ཧུབ་ཀྱིས་སྟོབ་པས with the ocean, my ལྒོབ is great.
Having put a mountain into a sesame seed, my deeds are great.[^T06-014][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-065][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-075][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-076][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-077][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-078][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-079]

<!-- pair: MTP-000349; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༨༥

[Printed page 485.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-080][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-081]

<!-- pair: MTP-000350; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ང་ཡིས་མུན་པ་གོས་སུ་གྱོན་པས་དཔའ་བོ་ཆེ
ང་ཡིས་འཁོར་འདས་གོམ་གཅིག་བགྲོད་པས་མཆོངས་པ་ཆེ
ང་ཡིས་ས་རྣམས་དུས་གཅིག་ནོན་པས་གཟི་བརྗིད་ཆེ
ང་ཡིས་ཉི་ཟླ་གདན་དུ་བཏིང་བས་བརྗིད་པོ་ཆེ

Having worn darkness as clothing, I am a great hero.
Having traversed saṃsāra and nirvāṇa in one stride, my leap is great.
Having pressed down the grounds at one time, my majesty is great.
Having spread out the sun and moon as a seat, my splendor is great.[^T06-015][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-078][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-082][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-083][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-084][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-085]

<!-- pair: MTP-000351; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ང་ལ་འཁོར་འདས་དུས་གཅིག་རྫོགས་པས་བྱ་དང་བྲལ
ང་ལ་སྣང་བ་ཐོགས་མེད་ཤར་བས་རང་ཤར་ཉིད
ང་ལ་རྟོགས་དང་མ་རྟོགས་མེད་པས་ཡེ་སངས་རྒྱས
ང་ལ་རེ་དང་དོགས་པ་མེད་པས་འཁོར་འདས་དུས་གཅིག་རྫོགས

Because saṃsāra and nirvāṇa are complete in me at one time, I am free from doing.
Because appearance arises unobstructedly in me, [it is] self-arising itself.
Because realization and non-realization are absent in me, [I am] primordially buddha.
Because hope and fear are absent in me, saṃsāra and nirvāṇa are complete at one time.[^T06-015][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-078][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-086][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-087][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-088][^T06-L005]

<!-- pair: MTP-000352; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༨༦

[Printed page 486.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-089][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-090]

<!-- pair: MTP-000353; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ང་ལ་ཡེ་ང་བག་ཚ་མེད་པས་འཇིགས་པ་ཀུན་དང་བྲལ
ང་ལ་འཆོས་པའི་མཁན་པོ་མེད་པས་ཡེ་ནས་ལྷུག་པ་ཉིད
ང་ལ་གཟུང་འཛིན་ཡུལ་དུ་མེད་པས་ཡེ་ནས་རང་གནས་ཉིད
ང་ལ་སྣོད་བཅུད་གཉིས་སུ་མེད་པས་ཐམས་ཅད་ང་རང་ཉིད
ང་ལ་ཆོས་ཅན་ཆོས་ཉིད་མེད་པས་ཐམས་ཅད་གཅིག་བའོ

Because ཡེ་ང and apprehension are absent in me, I am free from all fear.
Because there is no master of contriving in me, [there is] letting be from the beginning.
Because apprehended object and apprehending subject are not objects in me, [there is] self-abiding from the beginning.
Because receptacle and contents are not two in me, everything is myself.
Because bearers of phenomena and the nature of phenomena are absent in me, everything is one.”[^T06-016][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-091][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-092][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-093][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-094][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-095][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-096][^T06-L004]

<!-- pair: MTP-000354; format: prose; role: main_text -->
རྡོ་རྗེ་འཛིན་པས་ཡང་གསོལ་པ

The holder of the vajra again petitioned:[^T06-001][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-091]

<!-- pair: MTP-000355; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དེ་ལྟར་འཇིག་རྟེན་འཁོར་འདས་ཆོས
ཐམས་ཅད་ངོ་བོ་གཅིག་ཉིད་ན
སེམས་ཅན་གིས་བཅིངས་པ་ལགས

“If, in this way, all worldly phenomena of saṃsāra and nirvāṇa
are one in essence,
[—] is bound by sentient beings?[^T06-017][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-091][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-097][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-098][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-099][^T06-L001][^T06-L004]

<!-- pair: MTP-000356; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དེ་དག་འཆིང་བར་མི་རིགས་ན
ཡང་ན་མ་བཅིངས་དེ་ཉིད་དམ
འོན་ཏེ་གཞན་དག་གིས་བཅིངས་སམ
བཅོམ་ལྡན་འདས་ཀྱིས་བདག་ལ་སུངས

If it is unfitting that those are bound,
or is that itself unbound?
Or is [it] bound by others?
Bhagavān, སུངས to me.”[^T06-017][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-091][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-100][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-101][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-102][^T06-L001]

<!-- pair: MTP-000357; format: prose; role: main_text -->
བཅོམ་ལྡན་འདས་ཀྱིས་བཀའ་སྩལ་པ

The Bhagavān spoke:[^T06-001][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-091]

<!-- pair: MTP-000358; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སེམས་ཅན་ཉོན་མོངས་ལྔ་ཉིད་ནི

“As for sentient beings’ five afflictions,[^T06-018][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-091][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-103][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-104][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-105][^T06-L001]

<!-- pair: MTP-000359; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༨༧

[Printed page 487.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-106][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-107]

<!-- pair: MTP-000360; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ནམ་མཁའ་ལ་ནི་སྤྲིན་བཞིན་དུ
གློ་བུར་རྐྱེན་དང་བཅས་པ་ཉིད
སྤྲིན་དེ་ནམ་མཁའི་ངང་ལས་བྱུང
དེ་བཞིན་ནམ་མཁར་ཐིམ་པ་ན
དབྱེར་མེད་གཅིག་གི་ངང་ཞེས་བྱ

like clouds in space,
[they] are accompanied by adventitious conditions.
Those clouds arise from the state of space;
when they likewise dissolve into space,
[it] is called the state of inseparable oneness.[^T06-018][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-104][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-108][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-109][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-110]

<!-- pair: MTP-000361; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དེ་བཞིན་ཉོན་མོངས་དྲི་མ་མེད
རང་བྱུང་རང་བཞིན་ཉིད་ཤེས་ན
སེམས་ཅན་འཆིང་བར་ག་ལ་འགྱུར

Likewise, [there is] no stain of affliction.
If naturally arising intrinsic nature itself is known,
how could sentient beings be bound?[^T06-018][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-104][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-111][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-112][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-113][^T06-L001][^T06-L003]

<!-- pair: MTP-000362; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དེ་ཉིད་ལ་ནི་བདེན་བཟུང་ན
སངས་རྒྱས་ཉིད་ཀྱང་འཆིང་བར་འགྱུར
སེམས་ཅན་རྣམས་ནི་ཅིས་མི་འཆིང

If that itself is held as true,
even buddhas themselves become bound;
why would sentient beings not be bound?[^T06-019][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-104][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-114][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-115][^T06-L001]

<!-- pair: MTP-000363; format: verse; role: main_text -->
བདེན་མེད་རང་བཞིན་མེད་ཤེས་ན
དྲི་མ་རྣམས་ནི་རང་སར་དག
ཆུ་ཡི་རྙོག་མ་རང་དངས་ལྟར
ཆོས་ཀུན་རང་བཞིན་མེད་གཅིག

If [one] knows that there is no truth and no intrinsic nature,
the stains are purified in their own place.
Like turbidity in water becoming clear by itself,
all phenomena are one in lacking intrinsic nature.[^T06-019][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-104][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-116][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-117][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-118][^T06-L001][^T06-L002][^T06-L003][^T06-L004]

<!-- pair: MTP-000364; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དེ་ལྟར་དངོས་མེད་མ་ཤེས་ན
ཕུང་པོ་ཁམས་དང་སྐྱེ་མཆེད་ལ
གཟུང་འཛིན་དག་གིས་བཟུང་ན་ནི

If [one] does not know nonentity in this way,
and, regarding the aggregates, elements, and sense bases,
[one] holds through apprehended object and apprehending subject,[^T06-020][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-104][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-119][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-120][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-121][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-122][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-123][^T06-L004]

<!-- pair: MTP-000365; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༨༨

[Printed page 488.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-124][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-125]

<!-- pair: MTP-000366; format: verse; role: main_text -->
འཁོར་བ་ཉིད་ལ་ཡུན་རིང་གནས
ཁམས་གསུམ་པ་ཡི་ཁང་པ་རུ
མིང་དང་གཟུགས་ཀྱི་བཙོན་པར་ཚུད
མ་རིག་ལས་ཀྱི་ལྕགས་ཀྱིས་བསྡམས

[one] abides for a long time in saṃsāra itself.
In the house of the three realms,
[one] enters the prison of name and form,
bound with the iron of ignorance and action,[^T06-020][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-122][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-126][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-127][^T06-L004][^T06-L005]

<!-- pair: MTP-000367; format: verse; role: main_text -->
རང་བྱུང་སྒྲོན་མ་ཉིད་དང་བྲལ
འཁོར་བའི་མུན་ནག་སྟུག་པོས་གཡོགས
འདོད་ཆགས་ལན་ཚའི་རོལ་ཆགས
གཏི་མུག་ཞགས་པས་དམ་དུ་བཅིངས

separated from the naturally arising lamp itself,
covered by the thick darkness of saṃsāra,
attached to the ལན་ཚའི་རོལ of desire,
bound tightly by the noose of deluded dullness,[^T06-021][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-122][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-128][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-129][^T06-L003][^T06-L004]

<!-- pair: MTP-000368; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཞེ་སྡང་མེ་ཡི་ཚད་པས་གདུངས
ང་རྒྱལ་དག་གིས་མགོ་གཏུམས་ཏེ
ཕྲག་དོག་ཉིད་ཀྱི་སྒོ་མོས་བསྒྲིབས

tormented by the heat of the fire of hatred,
with the head wrapped in pride,
obscured by the door of jealousy itself,[^T06-021][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-122][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-130][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-131][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-132]

<!-- pair: MTP-000369; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཁོང་ཁྲོ་ལ་སོགས་དམག་གིས་བསྐོར
དྲན་ཉིད་ཀྱིས་གཡས་གཡོན་བརྟན
གཟུང་འཛིན་ཞགས་པས་སྐེ་ནས་བཏགས

surrounded by an army of anger and the like,
secured on right and left by mindfulness itself,
and tied by the neck with the noose of apprehended object and apprehending subject.[^T06-021][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-122][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-133][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-134][^T06-L003]

<!-- pair: MTP-000370; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༨༩

[Printed page 489.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-135][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-136]

<!-- pair: MTP-000371; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སྔ་ཕྱི་བག་ཆགས་འདམ་དུ་བྱིང
རྣམ་སྨིན་ཉིད་དང་ལག་སྤྲེལ་ཏེ
ལས་ཀྱིས་མ་བུ་བར་དོ་དང
ལས་ཀྱིས་མ་བུ་མཚམས་སྦྱར་ནས

sinking into the mud of past and future habitual tendencies,
joining hands with ripening itself,
through action, mother and child, the intermediate state, and—
through action, having linked mother and child,[^T06-022][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-137][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-138][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-139][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-140][^T06-L006]

<!-- pair: MTP-000372; format: verse; role: main_text -->
གཅིག་ནས་གཅིག་ཏུ་ཟོ་ཆུ་བཞིན
བཟང་ངན་རིམ་པའི་ལུས་བླངས་ཏེ
སོ་སོའི་རིགས་སུ་སྐྱེ་བར་འགྱུར

from one to another, like buckets of water,
[one] takes bodies in good and bad stages
and is born into the respective families.[^T06-022][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-137][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-141][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-142][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-143]

<!-- pair: MTP-000373; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ངར་འཛིན་རབས་ལ་འབོགས་ནས་ནི
སྡུག་བསྔལ་རྒྱ་མཚོའི་གཏིང་དུ་བྱིང
ངན་སོང་ལྕགས་ཀྱུས་སྙིང་ནས་བཟུང
རང་གིས་རང་བཅིང་ཉོན་མོངས་དགྲ

Having འབོགས into generations of grasping at “I,”
[one] sinks into the depths of the ocean of suffering,
held at the heart by the hook of the lower destinies.
Oneself binds oneself: afflictions are the enemy.[^T06-023][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-137][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-144][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-145][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-146][^T06-L006]

<!-- pair: MTP-000374; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དམྱལ་བ་པར་ནི་མེ་ཆུར་སྣང
ཡི་དགས་རྣམས་ལ་བཀྲེས་དང་སྐོམ
བྱོལ་སོང་རྣམས་ལན་བུན་ལྟར

For hell beings, [it] appears as fire and water;
for hungry ghosts, [as] hunger and thirst;
for animals, like ལན་བུན;[^T06-024][^CH06-G001199][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-137][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-147]

<!-- pair: MTP-000375; format: verse; role: main_text -->
མི་རྣམས་ཕུང་པོ་ཁམས་དང་ནི
སྐྱེ་མཆེད་འབྱུང་བ་ལྔ་རུ་སྣང
དེ་ཡང་བདེ་སྡུག་བཏང་སྙོམས་སོ

for humans, [as] aggregates, elements,
sense bases, and the five elements.
That too is pleasure, suffering, and equanimity.[^T06-024][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-137][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-148][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-149][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-150][^T06-L007]

<!-- pair: MTP-000376; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ལྷ་མིན་རྣམས་ལ་གོ་མཚོན་དུ་སྣང་ཏེ
ལྷ་རྣམས་འདོད་པའི་ཡོན་ཏན་ནོ

For demigods, [it] appears as armor and weapons;
for gods, [as] the qualities of desire.[^T06-024][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-137][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-151][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-152]

<!-- pair: MTP-000377; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༩༠

[Printed page 490.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-153][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-154]

<!-- pair: MTP-000378; format: verse; role: main_text -->
གསང་བའི་བདག་པོ་རྡོ་རྗེ་འཛིན
སེམས་ཅན་འཁོར་བ་དེ་དག་ནི
རང་གི་རྣམ་རྟོག་གིས་བཅིངས་ཀྱང
རང་བཞིན་མེད་པས་གྲོལ་བར་ངེས
ཆོས་ཀྱི་དབྱིངས་སུ་མཉམ་པའི་ཕྱིར

Lord of Secrets, holder of the vajra!
Those sentient beings in saṃsāra,
although bound by their own differentiating conceptualization,
are certain to be liberated because [it] lacks intrinsic nature,
for, in the basic space of phenomena, [they] are even.[^T06-025][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-152][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-155][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-156][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-157][^T06-L006][^T06-L008]

<!-- pair: MTP-000379; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཕུང་པོ་ལྔ་ཁམས་དང་སྐྱེ་མཆེད་ནི
རིགས་དང་རིགས་ཀྱི་བདག་པོར་གྲོལ
གཟུང་དང་འཛིན་པའི་ཆོས་ཅན་ནི
ཐབས་དང་ཤེས་རབ་ཉིད་དུ་གྲོལ

The five aggregates, elements, and sense bases
are liberated as families and lords of the families.
The bearers of phenomena of apprehended object and apprehending subject
are liberated as means and discerning knowing itself.[^T06-025][^CH06-G001216][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-152][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-158][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-159][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-160][^T06-L007]

<!-- pair: MTP-000380; format: verse; role: main_text -->
འཁོར་བ་ཆོས་ཀྱི་དབྱིངས་སུ་གྲོལ
ཆོས་ཉིད་གཞལ་ཡས་ཟང་ཐལ་ཉིད
དོན་དམ་ངེས་མེད་ཡངས་པ་ཆེ
ཡེ་ཤེས་འཕྲུལ་གྱི་ལྡེ་མིག་མངའ

Saṃsāra is liberated into the basic space of phenomena.
The nature of phenomena is immeasurable, unimpeded penetration itself.
The ultimate is unfixed, greatly open.
[One] possesses the magical key of primordial knowing.[^T06-026][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-152][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-161][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-162][^T06-L008][^T06-L010]

<!-- pair: MTP-000381; format: verse; role: main_text -->
འོད་གསལ་སྟོང་པའི་མར་མེ་གསལ
རིག་པ་རང་སྣང་འོད་པོ་ཆེ
ཆགས་པས་མ་བཅིངས་སྦྲུལ་མདུད་བཞིན

The lamp of clear light and emptiness is clear.
Awareness, self-appearance, is a great light.
[One] is not bound by attachment, like a snake’s knot.[^T06-026][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-152][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-163][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-164][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-165][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-166][^T06-L008]

<!-- pair: MTP-000382; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༩༡

[Printed page 491.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-167][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-168]

<!-- pair: MTP-000383; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཉམས་མྱོང་བདེ་བའི་རོ་ལ་ཆགས
ཐུགས་རྗེའི་ཞགས་པས་རང་ལུས་བཅིངས
ཤེས་རབ་མེ་ཡིས་ཉོན་མོངས་བསྲེག
འོད་གསལ་ཆེན་པོའི་གོས་ཀྱང་གྱོན
ཆོས་ཉིད་མཛོད་ཀྱི་སྒོ་ཕྱེ་སྡེ

Attached to the flavor of bliss in experiential acquaintance,
[one’s] own body is bound by the noose of compassionate responsiveness.
The fire of discerning knowing burns the afflictions;
[one] also wears the clothing of great clear light.
Having opened the treasury door of the nature of phenomena—[^T06-027][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-165][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-169][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-170][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-171][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-172][^T06-L006][^T06-L008]

<!-- pair: MTP-000384; format: verse; role: main_text -->
རང་རིག་མཚོན་ཐོགས་དམག་གི་དཔུང
ལྟ་སྤྱོད་གཉིས་ལ་ལུས་བརྟེན་ཏེ
རྟོག་པའི་ཞགས་པས་འཁོར་བར་བཅིངས

an army’s force bears the weapon of self-awareness;
the body rests on the two, view and activity;
with the noose of conceptual thought, [one] is bound in saṃsāra.[^T06-027][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-165][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-173][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-174][^T06-L008][^T06-L010]

<!-- pair: MTP-000385; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཆགས་པའི་རང་རིག་སྔ་ཕྱི་སྣང
རྟོག་མེད་ཆོས་དང་ལག་པ་སྒྲེལ
ཆོས་ཉིད་མ་བུ་གཉིས་སུ་འཕྲད
རང་རིག་ཡེ་ཤེས་གཅིག་པུ་ཉིད

The self-awareness of attachment appears before and after;
the hand is joined with phenomena free from conceptualization.
The two—mother and child of the nature of phenomena—meet.
Self-awareness, primordial knowing, is unique.[^T06-027][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-165][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-175][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-176][^T06-L008]

<!-- pair: MTP-000386; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སྟོང་གསལ་ཆེན་པོའི་ཡུལ་རྙེད་ནས
འཛིན་པ་མེད་པའི་ཆོས་སྐུ་ཐོབ
ཏིང་ངེ་འཛིན་རྒྱུན་ཆགས་རབ་ལས་བརྒལ
སྡུག་བསྔལ་རྒྱ་མཚོའི་ཀློང་ལས་ཐར

Having found the object of great emptiness and clarity,
[one] attains dharma embodiment without an apprehending subject,
goes beyond the excellence of continuous deep absorption,
and escapes from the expanse of the ocean of suffering.[^T06-028][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-165][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-177][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-178][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-179][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-180][^T06-L006][^T06-L008]

<!-- pair: MTP-000387; format: verse; role: main_text -->
གསང་སྔགས་ཐབས་ཀྱི་དགོངས་པ་གཅིག
ཡེ་ཤེས་ཉིད་ལ་ཡེ་ཤེས་རྫོགས

The enlightened intent of the means of secret mantra is one;
primordial knowing is complete in primordial knowing itself.[^T06-028][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-165][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-181][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-182][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-183]

<!-- pair: MTP-000388; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༩༢

[Printed page 492.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-184]

<!-- pair: MTP-000389; format: verse; role: main_text -->
མ་རིག་པ་ཉིད་རྣམ་དག་པས
ཆོས་སྐུ་འོད་གསལ་ཆེན་པོར་གནས
བདེ་བ་ཆེན་པོའི་རང་བཞིན་ལ
དངོས་དང་དངོས་མེད་མྱ་ངན་འདས

Because ignorance itself is completely purified,
[one] abides in dharma embodiment, great clear light.
Within the intrinsic nature of great bliss,
entities and nonentities have passed beyond sorrow.[^T06-029][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-182][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-185][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-186][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-187][^T06-L009]

<!-- pair: MTP-000390; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སྟོང་པ་ཡེ་ཤེས་ངོ་བོས་སྟོང
རང་བཞིན་ཀུན་ཁྱབ་ཡེ་ཤེས་ནི
སྒྲ་དང་དྲི་རོ་མྱ་ངན་འདས

Empty primordial knowing is empty in essence.
As for all-pervading intrinsic nature, primordial knowing,
sound, smell, and taste have passed beyond sorrow.[^T06-029][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-182][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-188][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-189][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-190][^T06-L009]

<!-- pair: MTP-000391; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སྤྲོས་པ་མེད་པའི་ཆོས་སྐུ་ནི
གཟུགས་དང་རྣམ་ཤེས་མྱ་ངན་འདས
རིག་པའི་ཡེ་ཤེས་ཁྱབ་གདལ་བས
སྟོང་གསུམ་ཐམས་ཅད་མྱ་ངན་འདས

As for dharma embodiment without conceptual elaborations,
form and consciousness have passed beyond sorrow.
Because the primordial knowing of awareness pervades expansively,
the entire three-thousandfold [world] has passed beyond sorrow.[^T06-029][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-182][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-191][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-192][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-193][^T06-L009]

<!-- pair: MTP-000392; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཆོས་དབྱིངས་ཡེ་ནས་རྣམ་དག་པས
འབྱུང་བ་ལྔ་ཉིད་མྱ་ངན་འདས
སངས་རྒྱས་སྐུ་གཅིག་ཀུན་ཁྱབ་པས
མིང་དང་ཡི་གེ་མྱ་ངན་འདས

Because the basic space of phenomena is completely purified from the beginning,
the five elements themselves have passed beyond sorrow.
Because the single embodiment of buddhas pervades all,
names and letters have passed beyond sorrow.[^T06-029][^CH06-G001256][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-182][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-194][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-195][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-196][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-197][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-198][^T06-L006][^T06-L007][^T06-L009]

༤༩༣

[Printed page 493.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-199]

<!-- pair: MTP-000394; format: verse; role: main_text -->
མི་ཤིགས་རྡོ་རྗེ་ཡེ་ཤེས་པས
ས་རྡོ་རི་བྲག་མྱ་ངན་འདས
ཆོས་ཉིད་སྤྲོས་དང་བྲལ་བ་ལ
ཐ་སྙད་ཉིད་ནི་མྱ་ངན་འདས

Through indestructible vajra primordial knowing,
earth, stones, mountains, and rocks have passed beyond sorrow.
In the nature of phenomena, free from conceptual elaborations,
conventional designation itself has passed beyond sorrow.[^T06-029][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-200][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-201][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-202][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-203][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-204][^T06-L010]

<!-- pair: MTP-000395; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཨེ་མ་ཧོ
འདི་ནི་རྫོགས་པའི་སངས་རྒྱས་ཀྱི
ངོ་བོ་ཉིད་ལས་མ་གཡོས་པའི
མི་འགྱུར་ཆོས་ཉིད་རབ་ཏུ་བརྟན

Emaho!
This, from the perfectly [awakened] buddhas’
very essence, has not moved:
the unchanging nature of phenomena, utterly stable.[^T06-030][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-200][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-205][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-206][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-207]

<!-- pair: MTP-000396; format: verse; role: main_text -->
རང་སྣང་རང་གྲོལ་རང་གི་ཡུལ
རང་བཞིན་རང་དག་རང་གི་གདངས
ཅོག་བཞག་བཞག་རང་བཞག་གོ

Self-appearance, self-liberation, one’s own object;
intrinsic nature, self-purified, one’s own radiance;
leaving as it is—leave, leave in itself.”[^T06-030][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-200][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-208][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-209][^T06-L008]

<!-- pair: MTP-000397; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དེ་ནས་རྡོ་རྗེ་ཅན་རྣམས་ཀྱིས
བཅོམ་ལྡན་འདས་ལ་འདི་སྐད་གསོལ

Then those possessing vajras
petitioned the Bhagavān in these words:[^T06-031][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-200][^T06-L006]

<!-- pair: MTP-000398; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཀྱེ་མ་བཅོམ་ལྡན་རྡོ་རྗེ་འཆང
འཇིག་རྟེན་སེམས་ཅན་འདི་ཀུན་གྱི
གནས་པའི་ས་ནི་ཇི་ལྟ་བུ
འགྲོ་བའི་ལམ་ནི་གང་ལྟར་ལགས
འབྲས་བུ་ཇི་ལྟ་བུ་ཞིག་ཐོབ
རྡོ་རྗེ་འཆང་གིས་བདག་ལ་གསུངས

“Alas, Bhagavān Vajradhara!
For all these worldly sentient beings,
what is the ground on which they abide like?
What is the path along which they go like?
What sort of result do they attain?
Vajradhara, speak to me.”[^T06-031][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-200][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-210][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-211][^T06-L006]

<!-- pair: MTP-000399; format: prose; role: main_text -->
བཅོམ་ལྡན་འདས་ཀྱིས་བཀའ་སྩལ་པ

The Bhagavān spoke:[^T06-031][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-200][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-212][^T06-L006]

<!-- pair: MTP-000400; format: verse; role: main_text -->
རྡོ་རྗེ་འཛིན་པ་ལེགས་ཉོན་ཅིག
རྟོག་བཅས་འཁྲུལ་པའི་ལུས་འདི་ལ

“Holder of the vajra, listen well!
In this body of delusion accompanied by conceptualizing,[^T06-031][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-200][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-213][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-214][^T06-L006]

<!-- pair: MTP-000401; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༩༤

[Printed page 494.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-215]

<!-- pair: MTP-000402; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དྲི་མེད་དག་པ་ཆོས་ཀྱི་སྐུ
མ་བཙལ་བཞག་པའི་ཚུལ་དུ་གནས

stainless and pure dharma embodiment
abides in the manner of being left without being sought.[^T06-032][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-216][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-217][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-218][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-219][^T06-L009]

<!-- pair: MTP-000403; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སངས་རྒྱས་རྣམས་ཀྱི་སྐུ་ཉིད་ནི
ཐུགས་ཀྱི་དཀྱིལ་འཁོར་མ་བསྐྱེད་རྫོགས
འགྱུར་བ་མེད་པའི་སྐུ་གཅིག་ནི
ཀུན་ཏུ་ཁྱབ་པའི་ཚུལ་དུ་གནས

As for the embodiments of buddhas themselves,
the mandala of awakened mind is complete without being generated.
As for the single embodiment without change,
[it] abides in an all-pervading manner.[^T06-032][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-216][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-220][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-221][^T06-L006]

<!-- pair: MTP-000404; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཡོངས་ལ་ཁྱབ་པའི་སེམས་གཅིག་པོ
རང་བཞིན་གཉུག་མའི་ངང་ལ་གནས
རྡོ་རྗེ་ལུ་གུ་རྒྱུད་ཀྱི་སྐུ
རྒྱུན་ནི་ཆད་པ་མེད་པར་གནས

The single ordinary mind that pervades everywhere
abides in the genuine state of intrinsic nature.
The embodiment of vajra—vajra chains—
abides with its continuity unbroken.[^T06-033][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-216][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-222][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-223][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-224][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-225]

<!-- pair: MTP-000405; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ལུ་གུ་རྒྱུད་ནི་གྲངས་མང་བས
ཆ་ཤས་སྐུ་ནི་བསམ་མི་ཁྱབ

Because vajra chains are many in number,
the embodiments of their portions are inconceivable.[^T06-033][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-216][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-226]

<!-- pair: MTP-000406; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཡོད་པ་གཅིག་ལས་མེད་པ་ལ
སྣང་བ་ཐམས་ཅད་ཡོངས་ལ་སྣང
སྟོང་དང་གསལ་བའི་བདག་ཉིད་ཅན

In that there is nothing other than one,
all appearances appear everywhere,
endowed with the identity of emptiness and clarity.[^T06-034][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-216][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-227][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-228][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-229][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-230][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-231]

<!-- pair: MTP-000407; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༩༥

[Printed page 495.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-232]

<!-- pair: MTP-000408; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དེ་ནི་ཡེ་ཤེས་སྣང་བའོ
སྐུ་ཡི་ངོ་བོ་དེ་བཞིན་ནོ

That is the appearance of primordial knowing.
The essence of embodiment is like that.[^T06-034][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-230][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-233][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-234]

<!-- pair: MTP-000409; format: verse; role: main_text -->
རྩ་རྣམས་ནས་ནི་ཐིག་ལེ་སྟེ
ཕྲ་དང་འཁྱུག་པའི་བདག་ཉིད་ཅན
འོད་ཟེར་ཉིད་ཀྱིས་མཛེས་པའོ

From the channels, there are spheres,
endowed with a fine and darting identity,
beautiful with rays of light.[^T06-035][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-230][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-235][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-236]

<!-- pair: MTP-000410; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དེ་ཡང་ཆ་ནི་གསུམ་པོ་སྟེ
གཡས་དང་གཡོན་དང་དབུས་མའོ

These have three parts:
right, left, and center.[^T06-035][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-230][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-237]

<!-- pair: MTP-000411; format: verse; role: main_text -->
གཡས་པ་རོ་མ་ཀུན་རྫོབ་ཐིག་ལེ་སྟེ
བདེ་བ་ཆེན་པོའི་ཡན་ལག་ཆ
བདེ་བ་འདུས་པའི་འབྱུང་གནས་ནི
སངས་རྒྱས་ཀུན་གྱི་གསང་ཆེན་ཡིན
ཐབས་ཀྱིས་མཚོན་པའི་རང་བཞིན་ནོ

The right, Roma, is the conventional sphere:
a part of the limbs of great bliss.
The source of gathered bliss
is the great secret of all buddhas;
[it is] intrinsic nature indicated by means.[^T06-035][^CH06-G001306][^CH06-G001307][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-230][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-238][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-239][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-240][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-241][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-242][^T06-L011]

<!-- pair: MTP-000412; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དོན་དམ་ཆོས་སྐུའི་རང་བཞིན་ཅན
སྟོང་གསལ་ཐིག་ལེ་གཅིག་ཏུ་གནས

Endowed with the intrinsic nature of ultimate dharma embodiment,
[it] abides as one sphere of emptiness-clarity.[^T06-035][^CH06-G001311][^CH06-G001312][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-230][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-243][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-244][^T06-L011]

<!-- pair: MTP-000413; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༩༦

[Printed page 496.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-245]

<!-- pair: MTP-000414; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དབུས་ནང་གནས་ཤིང་རང་བཞིན་གསལ
སྤྲོ་བསྡུ་སྨྲ་བསམ་ཡུལ་ལས་འདས
བགས་ཀྱིས་རྣམ་པར་རྟོག་པ་བྲལ
ཡོད་མེད་མཐའ་གཉིས་རྣམ་པར་གསལ

[It] abides within the center, and intrinsic nature is clear;
[it] is beyond the objects of proliferating and gathering, speaking and thinking.
Gently, [it is] free from differentiating conceptualization.
The two extremes of existence and nonexistence are completely clear.[^T06-036][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-246][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-247][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-248][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-249][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-250][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-251][^T06-L011][^T06-L012]

<!-- pair: MTP-000415; format: verse; role: main_text -->
རྟག་ཆད་མུ་ལ་གནས་པ་མེད
རྣམ་པར་རྟོག་མེད་ལྷུན་གྱིས་གྲུབ
བརྗོད་པ་མེད་ཅིང་ངང་གིས་སྟོང
དོན་དམ་དངོས་པོའི་ཐིག་ལེའོ

There is no abiding at the limits of permanence and annihilation.
Free from differentiating conceptualization, [there is] spontaneous presence.
Without expression, [it is] naturally empty.
[It is] the sphere of the ultimate entity.[^T06-036][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-246][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-252][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-253][^T06-L011][^T06-L015]

<!-- pair: MTP-000416; format: verse; role: main_text -->
གཡོན་ན་རང་བཞིན་ཐིག་ལེ་གནས
བདེ་བས་ཟུང་འཇུག་གཉིས་ཁྱབ་སྟེ
རབ་ཏུ་གནས་པ་མ་ཡིན་པར
ཁ་སྦྱར་ཉིད་ཀྱིས་བདེ་བ་ཐོབ

On the left abides the sphere of intrinsic nature.
Through bliss, union pervades both;
without abiding utterly,
through joining face to face, bliss is attained.[^T06-037][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-246][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-254][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-255][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-256][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-257][^T06-L011]

<!-- pair: MTP-000417; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཕྱག་རྒྱ་ཆེན་པོ་ཉིད་ཀྱི་རྒྱུ
བདེ་ཆེན་ཡེ་ཤེས་ཆེན་པོའི་ལམ
གསང་སྔགས་ཀུན་བདག་དཀྱིལ་འཁོར་ཡིན

[It is] the cause of Mahamudra itself,
the path of great bliss, great primordial knowing,
the mandala, lord of all secret mantra.[^T06-037][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-246][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-258][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-259][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-260][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-261][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-262][^T06-L011]

<!-- pair: MTP-000418; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༤༩༧

[Printed page 497.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-263]

<!-- pair: MTP-000419; format: verse; role: main_text -->
འཁོར་བ་བསྐྱབ་པའི་ཐབས་ཆེན་པོ
གསང་བའི་ཡེ་གདངས་འབར་བའི་འོད
དེ་ནི་བདེ་ཆེན་ངོ་བོའོ

[It is] the great means that rescues saṃsāra,
the light blazing with the primordial radiance of secrecy.
That is the essence of great bliss.[^T06-037][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-260][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-264][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-265][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-266][^T06-L011]

<!-- pair: MTP-000420; format: verse; role: main_text -->
འགྲོ་བའི་ལམ་ནི་མཐོང་བྱེད་སྒོ
དེ་ཉིད་གཡས་གཉིས་གཡོན་གཉིས་དབུས
སྒྲོན་མ་འབར་བའི་ཚུལ་དུ་གནས

The path of going is the gate that enables seeing.
That itself: two on the right, two on the left, and the center,
abides in the manner of a blazing lamp.[^T06-038][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-260][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-267][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-268][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-269][^T06-L012]

<!-- pair: MTP-000421; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཐིག་ལེ་སྟོང་པའི་སྒྲོན་མ་དང
དག་པ་དབྱིངས་ཀྱི་སྒྲོན་མ་དང
ཤེས་རབ་རང་བྱུང་ཉིད་དང་ནི
རྒྱང་ཞགས་ཆུ་ཡི་སྒྲོན་མ་ནི
མཐོང་བྱེད་ཡུལ་ལ་སྣང་བའོ

The lamp of the empty sphere,
the lamp of pure basic space,
naturally arising discerning knowing itself,
and the water lamp of the far-reaching lasso
appear to the objects of seeing.[^T06-038][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-260][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-270][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-271][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-272][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-273][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-274]

<!-- pair: MTP-000422; format: verse; role: main_text -->
རིག་པ་རྟོག་མེད་ལུ་གུ་རྒྱུད
བདེ་ཆེན་ཚངས་པའི་སྒོ་ནས་སྣང
དཔེར་ན་རྨ་བྱའི་མདངས་ལྟ་བུ

Awareness, free from conceptualization, vajra chains,
appears from the gate of Brahmā, great bliss,
like the luster of a peacock.[^T06-038][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-260][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-275][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-276][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-277]

<!-- pair: MTP-000423; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཐིག་ནི་འགྱུར་བ་ཡོད་མ་ཡིན

Thig: there is no change;[^T06-039][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-260][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-278][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-279][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-280][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-281]

༤༩༨

[Printed page 498.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-282]

<!-- pair: MTP-000425; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ལེ་ནི་ཁྱབ་ཅིང་ཡུལ་ལ་སྣང
སྒྲོན་མས་མུན་པ་སྣང་བར་བསྒྱུར

le pervades and appears to objects.
The lamp transforms darkness into appearance.[^T06-039][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-279][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-283][^T06-L012]

<!-- pair: MTP-000426; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དཔེར་ན་ནམ་མཁའི་འཇའ་ཚོན་ལྟར
དག་པས་ཉོན་མོངས་དྲི་མ་ཟད
དབྱིངས་ནི་ཡངས་ཤིང་རྒྱ་མ་ཆད
ཆོས་ཉིད་ཡུལ་ལ་རྣམ་དག་པས
གཉིས་ཀའི་ཟུར་ནས་འཆར་བར་བྱེད
ཁྱབ་བྱེད་མཐིང་ག་འགྱུར་བ་མེད

Like a rainbow in space,
through purity the stains of affliction are exhausted.
Basic space is vast, and its extent is unbroken.
Because the nature of phenomena is completely pure in the object,
[it] causes arising from the corners of both.
The pervading blue is unchanging.[^T06-040][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-279][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-284][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-285][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-286][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-287][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-288][^T06-L012]

<!-- pair: MTP-000427; format: verse; role: main_text -->
སྣང་བ་དག་པའི་རྐྱེན་ཕྲད་ན
ཁ་དོག་ལྔ་དང་ལྡན་པར་འགྱུར

If [it] meets the conditions of pure appearance,
[it] becomes endowed with five colors.[^T06-040][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-279][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-289][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-290][^T06-L012]

<!-- pair: MTP-000428; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དཔེར་ན་སྲེག་བྱེད་མེ་སྒྲོན་ལྟར
ཤེས་པས་ཆོས་རྣམས་གཅིག་ཆར་རྟོགས
རབ་ཀུན་གྱི་རྩེ་མོར་གྱུར
རང་ནི་གཞན་ལྟོས་ཆོས་དང་བྲལ
བྱུང་བ་མ་བཀག་ལྷུག་པར་སྣང
གཡོན་ནས་འཆར་བྱེད་སྒོ་མ་འགགས

Like a burning fire-lamp,
knowing realizes phenomena simultaneously.
Rab becomes the summit of all.
Rang is free from phenomena dependent on others.
Byung ba, unobstructed, appears in letting be.
From the left, the gate that enables arising is unceasing.[^T06-041][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-279][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-291][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-292][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-293][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-294][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-295][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-296][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-297][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-298][^T06-L012]

༤༩༩

[Printed page 499.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-299]

<!-- pair: MTP-000430; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དཔེར་འཛིན་བྱེད་ལྕགས་ཀྱུ་ལྟར
རྒྱུད་ནི་སྒོ་ལྔའི་རྟ་ཕོ་ལ
ཡིད་ཀྱི་རྣམ་ཤེས་ཞོན་ནས་འགྱུ
ཡུལ་རྣམས་ཀུན་ལ་འཇུག་ཅིང་སྡུད

Like a hook that apprehends,
the continuum, on the stallion of the five gates—
mental consciousness mounts and moves,
entering all objects and gathering.[^T06-042][^CH06-G001364][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-297][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-300][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-301][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-302][^T06-L012][^T06-L013][^T06-L015]

<!-- pair: MTP-000431; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཞགས་ནི་གཟུང་བའི་ཡུལ་ཉིད་ལ
འཛིན་པའི་སེམས་ཀྱིས་རྣམ་པར་གཟུང

“Lasso”: to the very objects of the apprehended object,
the ordinary mind of the apprehending subject fully apprehends.[^T06-042][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-297]

<!-- pair: MTP-000432; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ཆུ་ནི་དངས་སྙིགས་འབྱེད་པར་བྱེད
དེ་ཡང་གཉིས་ན་ཆ་མཉམ་སྟེ
མདོར་ན་དབང་པོའི་དངས་མ་ཡིན

“Water” distinguishes the clear from the sediment.
Moreover, in the two the portions are equal.
In brief, [it] is the refined essence of the faculties.[^T06-042][^CH06-G001371][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-297][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-303][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-304][^T06-L013]

<!-- pair: MTP-000433; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དཔེར་ན་གསེར་གྱི་ལུ་གུ་རྒྱུད
བཟོ་མཁས་ལེགས་པར་བརྒྱུས་པ་ལྟར
རིག་པས་ཐམས་ཅད་ཤེས་ཤིང་རིག
རྟོག་མེད་འཁོར་བས་གོས་པ་མེད
ལུ་གུ་རྒྱུད་ནི་འབྲེལ་ཆགས་སོ
བདེ་ཆེན་བུ་ག་རྟོག་མེད་ལམ

For example, like a gold chain
well strung by a skilled craftsman,
awareness knows and is aware of everything.
Free from conceptualization, [it] is unstained by saṃsāra.
Vajra chains are connected in succession.
The opening of great bliss is the path free from conceptualization.[^T06-043][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-297][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-305][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-306][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-307][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-308][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-309][^T06-L013]

<!-- pair: MTP-000434; format: verse; role: main_text -->
འབྲས་བུ་རྣལ་བ་གཉིས་ཞེས་ཏེ
མ་སྨིན་འཁོར་བ་ཉིད་དང་ནི
གྲོལ་བྱེད་སྐུ་དང་ཡེ་ཤེས་སོ

The result is said to be two རྣལ་བ:
unripened saṃsāra itself,
and the liberating embodiments and primordial knowing.[^T06-044][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-297][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-310][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-311][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-312][^T06-L013]

<!-- pair: MTP-000435; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དེ་ཡང་སྐྱེ་བཞིས་བསྡུས་པ་ཡི

Those, moreover, are encompassed by the four births:[^T06-044][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-297]

<!-- pair: MTP-000436; format: prose; role: source_metadata -->
༥༠༠

[Printed page 500.][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-313]

<!-- pair: MTP-000437; format: verse; role: main_text -->
ལྷ་དང་ལྷ་མིན་མི་རྣམས་དང
བྱོལ་སོང་རྣམས་དང་ཡི་དྭགས་དང
དེ་བཞིན་དམྱལ་བའི་འཇིག་རྟེན་ནི
མ་སྨིན་པ་ལས་གཟུགས་སུ་སྣང

gods, demigods, humans,
animals, hungry ghosts,
and likewise the world of hells
appear as forms from the unripened.[^T06-044][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-314][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-315][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-316][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-317][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-318][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-319][^T06-L013]

<!-- pair: MTP-000438; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དེ་དག་འདོད་ཆགས་ཞེ་སྡང་དང
ང་རྒྱལ་གཏི་མུག་ཕྲག་དོག་སོགས
ཉོན་མོངས་དྲི་མའི་རྩ་བ་ཅན
སྟོང་ཕྲག་དུ་མས་བསྡུས་པའོ

Those have desire and hatred,
pride, deluded dullness, jealousy, and the like
as the roots of the stains of affliction;
[they] are encompassed by many thousands.[^T06-045][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-314][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-320][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-321][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-322][^T06-L014]

<!-- pair: MTP-000440; format: verse; role: main_text -->
མངོན་སྣང་ཡུལ་ནི་འདི་ལྟར་རོ
སྐུ་གསུམ་དང་ནི་ཡེ་ཤེས་ལྔ
སྟོང་པ་ཉིད་དང་གསལ་བས་ཁྱབ
ཐིག་ལེ་འདུ་འབྲལ་མེད་པའི་ཕྱིར
ཚིག་མེད་ཉིད་དང་རྗོད་བྱེད་བྲལ

The object of manifest appearance is like this:
three embodiments and five primordial knowings
are pervaded by emptiness and clarity.

[there are] no utterances, and [it is] free from what expresses.[^T06-046][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-314][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-325][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-326][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-327][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-328][^T06-L014]

<!-- pair: MTP-000445; format: verse; role: main_text -->

བདེན་པའི་ཁྱད་པར་རེ་རེ་ལས
ཡུལ་གྱི་ཤེས་པ་རེ་རེ་ཡིས
རྟོགས་པའི་ཚད་ནི་རེ་རེ་རྙེད

From each of the gates that enable arising,

<!-- pair: MTP-000446; format: verse; role: main_text -->
དེ་ནས་སྣང་བ་བརྟས་ནས་ནི
རིམ་གྱིས་ཆོས་ཉིད་ཀ་དག་མཐོང
དེས་ཀྱང་འབྲས་བུ་བདེ་ཆེན་ཐོབ
སངས་རྒྱས་ཀུན་གྱི་གསང་བ་གཅིག

རང་བཞིན་དུ་ནི་རྣམ་པར་གནས

<!-- pair: MTP-000448; format: verse; role: main_text -->

དེ་ཉིད་འབྲས་བུ་ལ་གནས་པའོ

That, in its result—
entity itself and nonentity too—

<!-- pair: MTP-000449; format: verse; role: main_text -->

ངོ་བོ་དེ་ཡི་མཚན་ཉིད་ཡིན

ངོ་བོ་ཉིད་ཀྱི་རང་ལུས་ཡིན
ཅོག་བཞག་དང་ནི་རང་སོ་ཉིད
ངོ་བོ་ཉིད་ཀྱི་བསམ་གཏན་ཡིན

སེམས་ཅན་གྱི་སྣང་བ་ཐབས་ལ་མཁས་པ་བསྟན་པའི་ལེའུ་སྟེ་དྲུག་པའོ

Thus, from the precious secret tantra String of Pearls, the sixth chapter, teaching skill in means concerning the appearances of sentient beings.[^T06-049][^CH06-G001426][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-352][^MTP-AUDIT-C06-358]

## Endnotes

[^T06-001]: S1053–S1060, repeated speaker formulas S1143/S1151: རྡོ་རྗེ་འཛིན་པས is provisionally “the holder of the vajra,” distinct in form from རྡོ་རྗེ་འཆང (“Vajradhara”); བཅོམ་ལྡན་འདས is retained as “Bhagavān,” and གསང་སྔགས་བདག is “Lord of Secret Mantra.” Unlisted whole expressions སངས་རྒྱས, སེམས་ཅན, ཉོན་མོངས and དྲི་མ are provisionally “buddha,” “sentient being,” “affliction” and “stain.” The five afflictions are not named in this opening question. None of these working uses changes the canonical glossary or shortens standalone ordinary mind. Review these technical additions and titles before approving any default.
[^T06-002]: S1055–S1056: སངས་རྒྱས་ཐམས་ཅད་གཅིག་ཉིད་པའི / དེ་ཉིད་དགོངས་པ་ཇི་ལྟར་ལགས has a genitive first line and an elliptical second. “The oneness … that very enlightened intent” provisionally connects them without asserting that oneness and enlightened intent are separate entities. དེ་ཉིད could instead be read as a technical “suchness” here; that unlisted sense is not silently selected. Review the attachment. The golden reading corrects the transcript’s གོངས to printed དགོངས; source history is recorded separately.
[^T06-003]: S1061–S1066: རང་བཞིན་ནི་ངོ་བོ་ཡིས / དེ་ཉིད་ལས་ནི་ཁྱད་པར་ཕྱེ is syntactically compressed. The working lines take intrinsic nature as what is distinguished and ངོ་བོ་ཡིས instrumentally, but the antecedent of དེ་ཉིད and the differentiation remain unresolved; no doctrinal repair is supplied. In ངོ་བོ་ཡེ་ཤེས་སྣང་བ་ལ, “essence, primordial knowing” is a tentative apposition; a possessive relation is also possible. S1065 retains both all-pervasiveness and the explicit five embodiments without naming them. Review the clause joins and the relation of essence to primordial knowing.
[^T06-004]: S1067/S1069 continue across the page marker: སྣང་བ་ཉིད་ལ་ཟུང་འཇུག་སྟེ / སྟོང་པ་ལ་ནི་འོད་གསལ་ལོ. “Union” is provisional for unlisted ཟུང་འཇུག, not an abbreviation of the established union of calm abiding and insight. S1070 ཅིག་ཆར and S1071 རིམ་གྱིས are provisionally “simultaneous” and “gradual”; the source does not name the glossary’s simultaneous or gradual practitioner types. The supplied “[it is]” preserves the second line’s elliptical predicate. Review the union relation and its participants.
[^T06-005]: S1072–S1079: the repeated locative ལ is retained as “in”; the unstated subject of “beyond” is bracketed. མཐའ, རྡུལ, བདེ་ཆེན, དངོས་པོ and ཡུལ are provisionally “extreme,” “dust,” “great bliss,” “entity” and “domain” in these constructions. རང་བྱུང modifies the king of awareness as “naturally arising”; this is not the separately established whole expression naturally arising primordial knowing. Review the scope of “appearance” and “clear light” at S1074 and the implicit referent of S1079.
[^T06-006]: S1080–S1085: ང་ཉིད་སངས་རྒྱས་དགོངས་པ་ལས retains “from” for ལས; its relation to the ensuing first-person identifications is tentative. Unlisted འཁོར་བ / མྱང་འདས are “saṃsāra / nirvāṇa,” ཆོས / ཆོས་མིན “phenomena / non-phenomena,” ངན་སོང “lower destinies,” དམྱལ་བ “hells,” དཀྱིལ་འཁོར “mandalas,” ལྷ “deities,” སྔགས “mantra,” and དཀོན་མཆོག “precious ones,” all provisional. No absent enumeration of precious ones is supplied. Review the opening ablative and these scoped additions; repetitive “I” remains part of the source’s assertion.
[^T06-007]: S1087–S1090: “teacher, retinue, teaching” represents སྟོན་པ་འཁོར་དང་བསྟན་པ. S1088 རང་གི་འཁོར་སྡུད་སྡུད་པ repeats the gathering form; the English retains the repetition rather than deleting an apparent duplication. དབང / དམ་ཚིག / སྡོམ་པ are provisional “empowerment / commitments / vows.” “Commitment” continues chapter 5’s declared local choice; chapter 4’s saved draft used provisional “samaya,” and neither is an approved glossary mapping. Review the gathering syntax and technical additions.
[^T06-008]: S1091–S1094: unlisted བསམ་གཏན is provisionally “meditative stability,” distinct from ཏིང་འཛིན, the attested shortened form rendered “deep absorption.” མཆོད་པ, ཁྲུས, གཙང་སྦྲ and དྲི་མེད are “offerings,” “washing,” “cleanliness” and “stainlessness.” དགེ / མི་དགེ are “virtuous / nonvirtuous,” modifying activity. The textual categories མདོ་སྡེ / འདུལ་བ / མངོན་པ are provisionally “sūtra discourses / discipline / higher doctrine.” These are scoped technical proposals, not activated entries; review the short absorption form and category names.
[^T06-009]: S1095–S1101: བདེན་གཉིས is “two truths”; the source explicitly associates empty and not empty with them without naming conventional or ultimate. S1096 སྤྱི་དང་ཐེག་པའི་ཁྱད་པར leaves the scope of “the general” compressed. སྡིག / ལས / རྣམ་སྨིན are provisionally “wrongdoing / action / ripening”; the karmic context does not change bare action into the canonical karmic-wind compound. སྣོད་བཅུད is literally represented by “receptacle and contents,” an explicit local alternative to chapter 5’s inactive “environment and inhabitants” proposal; neither is an approved mapping. S1099 preserves going and not going rather than silently selecting beings and non-beings. In S1100 ལས་དང་དེ་ཉིད་ཉོན་མོངས་དྲི་མ་ང, the attachment of “that itself” and “stain of affliction” is unresolved; the English retains all members. དབང་པོ / ཡུལ are provisionally “faculties / objects.” Review these joins and local senses.
[^T06-010]: S1103–S1106: ཕུང་པོ / ཁམས / སྐྱེ་མཆེད are provisional “aggregates / elements / sense bases”; no absent numerical list is supplied. ཡི་གེ and གཟུགས are “letters” and “forms”; canonical སྒྲ is retained as “words” beside names and letters. In གཟུང་འཛིན་ཡུལ་དང་བདག་རྟོག, the draft lists the complete apprehended-object/apprehending-subject pair, objects and “conceptualizing a self.” Their exact coordination is open; བདག་རྟོག is an unlisted construction, not automatically the canonical differentiating conceptualization. Review the object attachment and the self-conceptualizing construction.
[^T06-011]: S1107–S1111: སྡུག་བསྔལ, རྨོངས་པ, བདེ and བཏང་སྙོམས are provisionally “suffering,” “bewilderment,” “pleasure” and “equanimity.” “Pleasure” here belongs to the pleasure/suffering/equanimity list; the later བདེ་ཆེན construction remains “great bliss.” At S1108 སྒྲ is provisionally “sound” in the smell/taste/touch sensory list, a disclosed contextual departure from canonical “word,” which remains in S1105. ལོག་འཛིན is provisionally “mistaken apprehension,” not a replacement for the standalone apprehending-subject entry. Review the sensory exception and these unlisted categories.
[^T06-012]: S1112–S1115: “[things]” is a limited grammatical supply for ཇི་ལྟར་སྣང་བ. Fixed S1114 has ཕགས་པ; the separately attributed native audit records printed འཕགས་པ (MTP-AUDIT-C06-061). “Exalted beyond all” provisionally interprets the fixed form through that print evidence; the golden spelling is not changed. བཅོས་པའི་ཚིག is provisionally “contrived utterance,” preserving the contrivance family without claiming an exact canonical entry. Fixed S1115 རྟག་ཆད་ཉིས་མེད corresponds to printed རྟག་ཆད་གཉིས་མེད (audit 063). “Neither permanence nor annihilation” selects absence of both provisionally; “permanence and annihilation are not two” remains a possible scope. The orthographic evidence does not decide that semantic question. Review the negative scope and ཀུན་ལྟར (“in every manner”).
[^T06-013]: S1117–S1124: the first-person contrasts are retained even where they appear surprising. In particular, ཀུན་ལ་བརྟེན་པ at S1121 is “I depend on all,” not an unmarked reversal to “all depend on me.” S1118 ཐམས་ཅད་གཏིང་རྫོགས is provisionally “complete to the depths of everything.” S1123 བེམ་རིག་གཉིས་མེད is provisionally “insentient and aware are not two”; absence of both is another possible scope. “Aware” realizes the awareness family predicatively, while བེམ remains an unlisted technical member. The high/low and unique/all contrasts are not harmonized. Review the compressed depth expression and negative scope.
[^T06-014]: S1125–S1127: རི་བོ་རྒྱ་ཐེག་བྱས་པས and རྒྱ་མཚོ་ཧུབ་ཀྱིས་སྟོབ་པས་ལྒོབ་ཆེ contain unresolved forms. རྒྱ་ཐེག is retained rather than reconstructed as an expected carrying or weighing phrase. ཧུབ་ཀྱིས suggests a gulp-related construction, but the exact predicate སྟོབ and result ལྒོབ remain unresolved and are retained. The ocean, the agency of “I,” and “great” remain represented. S1127’s mountain entering a sesame seed is translated without treating it as a license to reconstruct the preceding marvels. Review the fixed forms and their source explanation; no alternate spelling has been inserted.
[^T06-015]: S1129–S1136: each པས causal construction is retained. འཁོར་འདས is provisionally the ordered pair “saṃsāra and nirvāṇa.” ས་རྣམས is “grounds” in the pressing-down line, without naming doctrinal levels. གཟི་བརྗིད / བརྗིད are provisionally “majesty / splendor” so their forms remain recoverable. S1134’s bracketed “[it is]” refers provisionally to arising appearance; S1135’s “[I am]” continues the speaker’s refrain. ཡེ་སངས་རྒྱས is “primordially buddha,” not a silent new glossary assignment. Review the implicit predicates and their reference.
[^T06-016]: S1138–S1142: fixed ཡེ་ང in ཡེ་ང་བག་ཚ་མེད is retained; it is not silently repaired to a familiar apprehension expression. བག་ཚ is provisionally “apprehension.” S1139 འཆོས་པའི་མཁན་པོ is “master of contriving,” not the separately listed contriver form; ལྷུག་པ retains “letting be.” S1140 གཟུང་འཛིན་ཡུལ་དུ་མེད is tentatively predicative, “are not objects in me”; absence of their objects is another possible attachment. རང་གནས is provisional “self-abiding.” S1141 leaves receptacle and contents as “not two”; S1142 instead follows its absent pair marker with “are absent.” ཆོས་ཅན is provisionally “bearers of phenomena,” distinguished from canonical nature of phenomena. Review the retained first form and these negative relations.
[^T06-017]: S1144–S1150: the fixed S1146 is སེམས་ཅན་གིས་བཅིངས་པ་ལགས, with གིས and no overt interrogative for “by what.” The draft therefore retains the instrumental “by sentient beings” and marks its missing/unclear subject “[—]”; it does not silently insert ཅིས or reverse the participants into “what binds sentient beings?” The next lines ask whether those are bound, whether that itself is unbound, or whether another binds it; their pronoun reference remains open. S1150 has fixed སུངས rather than the clearer གསུངས of S1059; it is retained, with “speak” only a possible reconstruction. Review both fixed forms and the questions’ participants.
[^T06-018]: S1152–S1161: the first line continues across the page marker. “Sentient beings’ five afflictions” takes the juxtaposition as a possessive relation; the source has no explicit genitive after སེམས་ཅན. The cloud comparison is kept distinct from the conclusion about knowing. གློ་བུར / རྐྱེན / ངང / དབྱེར་མེད are provisionally “adventitious / condition / state / inseparable”; གློ་བུར qualifies རྐྱེན within the “accompanied by” construction, and no canonical natural-state compound is supplied. In S1159 the unstated “[there is]” follows ཉོན་མོངས་དྲི་མ་མེད. S1160 retains both “naturally arising” and intrinsic nature. Review the initial possessive and the relation of the negative sentence to the following condition.
[^T06-019]: S1162–S1168: བདེན་བཟུང is provisionally “held as true”; S1165 བདེན་མེད་རང་བཞིན་མེད retains the separate negations of truth and intrinsic nature. The conditional argument includes buddhas being bound; that statement is not removed as doctrinally unexpected. རང་སར་དག is provisionally “purified in their own place,” not substituted with the different canonical liberated-in-its-own-place expression. S1168 ཆོས་ཀུན་རང་བཞིན་མེད་གཅིག is tentatively “all phenomena are one in lacking intrinsic nature”; review the relation of the final “one” to the preceding negative phrase.
[^T06-020]: S1169–S1176: the conditional sentence spans the metadata pair. དངོས་མེད is provisionally “nonentity,” related to the working entity/nonentity family, not the canonical emptiness entry by automatic equivalence. The agent of knowing/holding is implicit and bracketed. གཟུང་འཛིན་དག་གིས is retained instrumentally with the complete canonical pair, although the construction’s agent relation is compressed. ཁམས་གསུམ is provisionally “three realms,” distinct from constituent “elements” in the aggregates/elements/sense-bases list. Name and form are both retained in the prison image; མ་རིག་ལས is provisionally ignorance and action, without supplying an absent conjunction in the Tibetan. Review the instrumental relation and compound attachment.
[^T06-021]: S1177–S1186: the prison sequence continues with the same implicit person. Fixed S1179 འདོད་ཆགས་ལན་ཚའི་རོལ་ཆགས leaves ལན་ཚའི་རོལ unresolved; the draft retains it, and does not silently change the final ལ of རོལ or reconstruct the salt/taste imagery it may suggest. Technical members are provisionally འདོད་ཆགས “desire,” གཏི་མུག “deluded dullness,” ཞེ་སྡང “hatred,” ང་རྒྱལ “pride,” ཕྲག་དོག “jealousy,” and ཁོང་ཁྲོ “anger”; deluded dullness remains distinct from canonical dullness for བྱིང་བ and from this chapter’s རྨོངས་པ “bewilderment.” Chapter 5 used provisional “bewilderment” for གཏི་མུག; the present form-sensitive choice is an explicit local alternative, not an approved replacement. S1185 དྲན་ཉིད is provisionally “mindfulness itself,” preserving the mindfulness family despite its uncomfortable role in binding; no replacement by an expected guard or demon is supplied. Review the retained desire phrase and the mindfulness form. The source’s nooses, neck and surrounding army are all represented.
[^T06-022]: S1188–S1194 continue the prison sequence. S1190/S1191 are fixed as ལས་ཀྱིས་མ་བུ་བར་དོ་དང / ལས་ཀྱིས་མ་བུ་མཚམས་སྦྱར་ནས; both “through action, mother and child” phrases remain in the English. The first line’s final conjunction and its relation to “having linked” are unresolved, shown by the dash. The native audit MTP-AUDIT-C06-140 records only one visible occurrence and an additional སྲིད་པའི, with root-versus-explanation allocation unresolved. That does not authorize deletion, insertion or rejoining of the fixed lines. “Mother and child” is not assigned a doctrinal referent here. ཟོ་ཆུ is provisionally “buckets of water”; a water-drawing cycle may be intended, but no apparatus is supplied in the English. རིགས is provisional “families” for the respective birth categories. Review the repeated construction and its participants.
[^T06-023]: S1195–S1198: ངར་འཛིན་རབས་ལ་འབོགས leaves the verb འབོགས unresolved and retained. ངར་འཛིན is provisionally “grasping at ‘I’,” and རབས “generations”; the intended relation might instead concern a succession or level of self-grasping, and is not settled. [^T06-024]: S1199–S1206: the appearance predicate is explicit at S1199, S1203 and S1205; bracketed repeats only carry that predicate through the catalogue. The two occurrences of “elements” in the human list distinguish ཁམས from the explicitly five འབྱུང་བ; the local forms remain recoverable here and in usage records. Unlisted དམྱལ་བ་པ, ཡི་དགས, བྱོལ་སོང, ལྷ་མིན and ལྷ are provisionally “hell beings,” “hungry ghosts,” “animals,” “demigods” and “gods.” S1206 འདོད་པའི་ཡོན་ཏན is kept as “qualities of desire” rather than expanded into a named sense-pleasure list. Review the animal line, catalogue subjects and category names.
[^T06-025]: S1208–S1216: གསང་བའི་བདག་པོ / རྡོ་རྗེ་འཛིན are provisionally “Lord of Secrets / holder of the vajra.” Canonical differentiating conceptualization remains in S1210. S1211’s unexpressed subject of lacking intrinsic nature is provisionally “[it],” referring to binding or its conceptual cause; reference back to beings is also possible. S1212 “even” realizes the evenness family in མཉམ་པའི, and the expanded genitive ཆོས་ཀྱི་དབྱིངས retains basic space of phenomena. S1214’s families and their lords are not enumerated. S1215 གཟུང་དང་འཛིན་པའི་ཆོས་ཅན provisionally makes the pair qualify bearers of phenomena; its precise scope remains reviewable. ཐབས is provisional “means,” kept distinct from canonical discerning knowing. Review the implicit subjects and that genitive relation.
[^T06-026]: S1217–S1223: གཞལ་ཡས, དོན་དམ, ངེས་མེད, འཕྲུལ, ལྡེ་མིག and མར་མེ are provisional “immeasurable,” “ultimate,” “unfixed,” “magical,” “key” and “lamp.” The implicit possessor is bracketed at S1220. S1221 འོད་གསལ་སྟོང་པའི preserves clear light and emptiness without silently substituting the separately named lamp of the empty sphere. S1222’s awareness/self-appearance relation is treated as apposition provisionally. S1223 explicitly says not bound by ཆགས་པ, here provisional “attachment,” and gives a snake-knot comparison; no self-untying action is added. Review these nominal relations and the comparison.
[^T06-027]: S1225–S1236 deliberately retains attachment and binding alongside liberating imagery. S1226 binds one’s own body with compassionate responsiveness; S1232 explicitly binds [one] in saṃsāra with conceptual thought. Neither is silently changed into release. S1229 fixed ཕྱེ་སྡེ is tentatively read as opening followed by a connective; the unusual སྡེ is not corrected to སྟེ. S1233 ཆགས་པའི་རང་རིག is “the self-awareness of attachment,” with its genitive preserved, not negated. Unlisted རང་རིག is provisional “self-awareness,” distinct from self-recognition. S1234’s joined hand and S1235’s mother/child meeting are retained without assigning doctrinal identities. Review this entire contrast and its unstated agents; apparent doctrinal tension is not evidence for repairing the source.
[^T06-028]: S1237–S1242: སྟོང་གསལ preserves the source’s emptiness-then-clarity order, rather than imposing the canonical reverse-order compound clarity-emptiness. S1241’s genitive chain relates enlightened intent to the means of secret mantra; it is not a new universal equation. Review the excellence/continuity phrase and genitive scope.
[^T06-029]: S1244–S1263: the repeated མྱ་ངན་འདས is provisionally rendered verbally, “have/has passed beyond sorrow,” so the listed entities remain its subjects; it is not silently reduced to a uniform noun “nirvāṇa.” S1247 retains entities and nonentities, and S1248 སྟོང་པ་ཡེ་ཤེས་ངོ་བོས་སྟོང is tentatively “empty primordial knowing is empty in essence,” preserving both emptiness expressions. S1254 སྟོང་གསུམ is provisional “three-thousandfold [world]”; “world” is supplied and no arithmetic cosmology is asserted. S1260’s པས construction is taken instrumentally; མི་ཤིགས་རྡོ་རྗེ is provisional “indestructible vajra.” S1263 ཐ་སྙད is “conventional designation.” Review the empty/essence relation, the apposition and the three-thousandfold expression.
[^T06-030]: S1264–S1270: ཨེ་མ་ཧོ is retained as “Emaho!” without importing an expanded acclamation. རྫོགས་པའི་སངས་རྒྱས is provisionally “perfectly [awakened] buddhas”; the bracket shows the supplied relation. The first genitive attaches the buddhas to their very essence; the outer not-moving phrase qualifies the unchanging nature of phenomena. The English carries the possessive across the line break to preserve that chain. The referent of “This” remains context-dependent. S1268/S1269 preserve the repeated self/own constructions and the separate canonical self-appearance, self-liberation, intrinsic nature, self-purified and radiance. Fixed S1270 ཅོག་བཞག་བཞག་རང་བཞག retains three resting/leaving expressions; the first is a differently written attested form provisionally linked to canonical leaving as it is, and the remaining repetitions are not deleted. Review the chain and the elliptical instructions.
[^T06-031]: S1271–S1281: the metrical narrative couplet remains two lines. རྡོ་རྗེ་ཅན is provisionally “those possessing vajras,” distinct from the holder-of-the-vajra title and Vajradhara. The plural petitioners then use singular བདག at S1278; “me” is retained rather than regularized to “us.” གནས་པའི་ས is “the ground on which they abide,” without identifying it with the canonical capitalized Ground for གཞི. S1281’s རྟོག་བཅས is provisionally “accompanied by conceptualizing,” preserving the conceptualizing family; its scope over body or delusion is open. The question’s three members—abiding ground, going path, attained result—remain separate. Review the singular/plural relation and the body modifier.
[^T06-032]: S1283–S1288: ཆོས་ཀྱི་སྐུ is a genitively expanded dharma-embodiment form, not a new gloss. S1284 མ་བཙལ་བཞག་པའི་ཚུལ preserves both not seeking and leaving. S1286 ཐུགས་ཀྱི་དཀྱིལ་འཁོར is provisionally “the mandala of awakened mind”; standalone ཐུགས has no approved glossary assignment, and the local respectful reading does not replace ordinary mind, enlightened intent or compassionate responsiveness elsewhere. The nominal relation between the buddhas’ embodiments and this mandala is tentative; no absent verb of production is supplied. Review the respectful term and clause attachment.
[^T06-033]: S1289–S1294: ordinary mind remains the complete canonical term. S1290 རང་བཞིན་གཉུག་མའི་ངང is tentatively “the genuine state of intrinsic nature”; it is not the exact canonical word order of genuine intrinsic nature. Their relation remains unresolved. S1292 རྒྱུན is provisionally “continuity,” distinct from canonical continuum for རྒྱུད. S1294 ཆ་ཤས་སྐུ is provisionally “embodiments of their portions”; the possessive is an interpretive supply referring to vajra chains. Review the explicit-vajra relationship and the parts/embodiments relation.
[^T06-034]: S1295–S1300: ཡོད་པ་གཅིག་ལས་མེད་པ་ལ is tentatively “in that there is nothing other than one,” taking ལས comparatively; the being/nonbeing construction is compressed and an alternative separation remains possible. S1297 སྟོང་དང་གསལ་བའི་བདག་ཉིད་ཅན preserves emptiness then clarity and provisionally renders བདག་ཉིད་ཅན “endowed with the identity of.” The phrase continues across the page marker into “that is the appearance of primordial knowing”; the page boundary does not close the sentence artificially. Review the comparison and identity relation.
[^T06-035]: S1301–S1312: རྩ is provisionally “channel,” and ཕྲ / འཁྱུག / འོད་ཟེར are “fine / darting / rays of light”; no named anatomy beyond the source is supplied. Fixed S1306 གཡས་པ་རོ་མ་ཀུན་རྫོབ་ཐིག་ལེ་སྟེ is provisionally segmented as “the right, Roma, is the conventional sphere.” The exact པ་རོ་མ sequence remains source-layer uncertain (native audit MTP-AUDIT-C06-239); this reading does not certify Roma as root text or authorize deleting it. S1307 ends in the corrected single ཆ, so “a part of the limbs” represents ཡན་ལག་ཆ without adding an absent ཅན. S1308’s gathering relation and S1310’s implicit copula are provisional. S1311/S1312 do not explicitly name their subject; it remains bracketed, and emptiness-clarity retains the order of སྟོང་གསལ. [^T06-036]: S1314–S1321: the unstated subject remains bracketed. སྤྲོ་བསྡུ is provisionally “proliferating and gathering,” a differently written construction from canonical འཕྲོ་འདུ, while སྨྲ་བསམ is speaking and thinking. Fixed S1317 ཡོད་མེད་མཐའ་གཉིས་རྣམ་པར་གསལ says the two extremes are completely clear; no unprinted elimination verb is supplied to harmonize it with the following negative. Review the first adverb and the clear/empty/entity sequence.

S1324 retains the negation of abiding utterly, and ཁ་སྦྱར is provisionally “joining face to face”; no absent participants or anatomical detail are added. [^T06-038]: S1333–S1343: S1334 explicitly has two on the right, two on the left, and the center; the draft does not silently reconcile that count with the following lamp list. S1338 has ཤེས་རབ་རང་བྱུང་ཉིད without an overt lamp noun, so only “naturally arising discerning knowing itself” is represented. S1339 is a genitively expanded form of the established water lamp of the far-reaching lasso. The seeing/object relation in S1340 is tentative. S1342 ཚངས་པའི་སྒོ is provisionally “gate of Brahmā,” with its relation to great bliss open; no physical location is supplied. S1343 མདངས is provisional “luster,” distinct in form from canonical radiance for གདངས. Review the count, shortened lamp expressions and the gate relation.
[^T06-039]: S1344/S1346 explain the two syllables of ཐིག་ལེ separately across the page marker. The draft therefore retains “Thig” for ཐིག and “le” for ལེ, while the undivided glossary term remains “sphere” elsewhere. These are local syllable explanations, not replacement glossary defaults. S1344’s “there is no change” retains འགྱུར་བ་ཡོད་མ་ཡིན. S1347 སྒྲོན་མས་མུན་པ་སྣང་བར་བསྒྱུར is provisionally “the lamp transforms darkness into appearance,” retaining the canonical appearance family rather than silently choosing illumination. Review the object relation and the lamp/appearance construction.
[^T06-040]: S1348–S1355: the exposition preserves the separately stated purity and basic-space components. S1351 ཆོས་ཉིད་ཡུལ་ལ་རྣམ་དག་པས is tentatively “because the nature of phenomena is completely pure in the object”; a different attachment of object and nature of phenomena remains possible. S1352 གཉིས་ཀའི་ཟུར identifies corners of “both” but does not explicitly name them; the bracketed subject of causing arising likewise remains open. “The pervading blue” retains ཁྱབ་བྱེད་མཐིང་ག rather than supplying an unstated colored entity. S1354–S1355 preserve the conditional and explicit five colors without naming them. Review the locative, the two referents and the cause of arising.
[^T06-041]: S1356–S1361 continue an apparent component explanation of ཤེས་རབ་རང་བྱུང. S1357 ཤེས་པས is instrumentally “knowing” in the working sentence; canonical realization remains distinct from conceptualizing. Fixed S1358 རབ་ཀུན་གྱི་རྩེ་མོར་གྱུར is retained as a “Rab” clause, rather than inserting a missing copula or syllable into the Tibetan. “Rang” exposes the self-component at S1359, and “Byung ba” the arising-component at S1360; their local meanings are proposals within the explanation, not replacements for the canonical whole lamp name or naturally arising primordial knowing. གཞན་ལྟོས is tentatively dependence on others, and མ་བཀག means unobstructed here. ལྷུག་པར retains letting be, while S1361 མ་འགགས retains the unceasing family. Review the compressed Rab clause and the component interpretation.
[^T06-042]: S1363–S1371: fixed/printed S1364 reads རྒྱུད, canonical “continuum,” not the different རྒྱང at the beginning of the lamp name. The draft retains that reading even in this apparent lamp-component exposition. Its relation to the stallion, mental consciousness and movement remains unresolved; the dash shows the compressed join rather than supplying an unprinted verb. The five gates are not enumerated. S1367 “Lasso” and S1369 “Water” mark the expressly isolated ཞགས / ཆུ components. The nominal apprehended object and apprehending subject remain visible in S1367/S1368; གཟུང is provisionally verbal “apprehends,” not an approved general substitution for the nominal glossary entry. S1368’s object/subject genitives remain awkward and open. S1370’s “two” has no supplied identity. དངས་མ is provisionally “refined essence,” distinct from canonical essence for ངོ་བོ. Review the continuum/stallion join, the apprehension relation and the two portions.
[^T06-043]: S1372–S1377: གསེར་གྱི་ལུ་གུ་རྒྱུད is provisionally “a gold chain” in the expressly non-visionary craftsman comparison. That scoped proposal does not globally replace canonical “vajra chains,” which is retained for ལུ་གུ་རྒྱུད in S1376’s awareness exposition. S1373 བརྒྱུས is provisionally “strung,” a distinct form from canonical continuum; བཟོ་མཁས is a skilled craftsman, not silently the canonical contriver. S1374 preserves knowing and being aware as separate verbs. S1375 retains free from conceptualization and the negated staining relation. འབྲེལ་ཆགས is provisional “connected in succession,” and S1377 བུ་ག “opening”; no absent anatomy is supplied. Review the gold-chain exception and the relation of the opening to the path.
[^T06-044]: S1378–S1386: fixed འབྲས་བུ་རྣལ་བ་གཉིས་ཞེས་ཏེ leaves རྣལ་བ unresolved and retained; it is not changed to an expected maturation word or dropped. The next two lines name unripened saṃsāra and liberating embodiments and primordial knowing, but the relation between those members and the retained form remains open. The “four births” in S1381 are not enumerated by name; the following list gives classes of beings and should not be forced into a four-item count. S1386 makes their appearance as forms arise “from the unripened,” leaving its nominal referent open. The source spelling ཡི་དྭགས is preserved in records, distinct from earlier ཡི་དགས; both receive the provisional hungry-ghost sense. Review the retained result form, the twofold division and the scope of the four births.
[^T06-045]: S1387–S1392: the desire/hatred/pride/deluded-dullness/jealousy list preserves its stated order and “the like.” S1390 སྟོང་ཕྲག་དུ་མས་བསྡུས is tentatively “encompassed by many thousands”; no absent unit or exact count is supplied. S1391–S1392’s genitive relation is provisionally carried across the English lines: ordinary mind and the like qualify apprehended object and apprehending subject, while mental faculty and the like remain additional instrumental members. Their exact attachment is open; no protected mind term is replaced by generic mind. Review the numerical scope and genitive/instrumental chain.
[^T06-046]: S1393–S1404: མངོན་སྣང is provisional “manifest appearance.” The explicit five primordial knowings are not named. S1397 ཚིག / རྗོད་བྱེད are provisionally “utterances / what expresses,” kept distinct from canonical word for སྒྲ. S1398/S1399 preserve the place of primordial purity free from stains and the manifest-buddha predicate, with the unstated subjects marked where needed. S1402 retains the repetition of conceptual elaborations and being unelaborated, not an imported general emptiness claim. S1404 has bare འབྲིང, rendered “the middling”; it does not explicitly include the noun faculty from the glossary’s middling-faculty expression. Its relation to the path and various appearances remains provisional. Review the implicit subjects and the middling referent.
[^T06-047]: S1405–S1412: each gate, each truth-distinction, each knowing of an object, and each measure of realization retain the repeated distributive རེ་རེ. The ནས and ལས phrases are represented as “from,” and the knowing phrase remains instrumental. The subject of gradual seeing and attainment is implicit and bracketed. S1409 བརྟས is provisionally “increases”; the passage does not explicitly name any of the canonical four visions, so their names are not substituted. Great bliss remains the result and “the single secret of all buddhas” a tentative apposition. Review the repeated ablatives and the final apposition.
[^T06-048]: S1413–S1425: the result passage retains stain and purity as two, followed by entity and nonentity, rather than harmonizing the lists. The referents of དེ་དག, དེ and དེ་ཡི in S1413/S1416/S1418 remain unresolved; the English preserves the repeated “those/that/its” structure and the interrupted nominal pair. S1419 preserves empty then clear, followed by aware and unchanging; no reverse-order clarity-emptiness compound is imposed. S1420 མཚན་ཉིད is provisional “defining characteristic,” distinct from essence. S1423 རང་ལུས is “own body,” not automatically the embodiment entry. S1424 ཅོག་བཞག is a differently written attested form provisionally linked to leaving as it is; རང་སོ is provisionally “one’s own condition,” with its precise sense open. བསམ་གཏན retains this chapter’s provisional “meditative stability.” Review the result references, own-condition phrase and final nominal relations.
[^T06-049]: S1426–S1427: the colophon’s title is provisionally “the precious secret tantra String of Pearls.” རྒྱུད is an explicit occurrence-specific “tantra” proposal in a textual title, while S1364 retains canonical “continuum”; neither replaces the other globally. སེམས་ཅན་གྱི་སྣང་བ་ཐབས་ལ་མཁས་པ་བསྟན་པ is tentatively “teaching skill in means concerning the appearances of sentient beings.” The relation of appearances to skill in means is compressed and remains reviewable. The source explicitly numbers this chapter sixth. Review the title construction and attachment; the fixed colophon remains separate from the preceding root verse.

[^MTP-AUDIT-C06-140]: Uncertain reading; source anchors MTP-S001190, MTP-S001191. Golden reading: `ལས་ཀྱིས་མ་བུ་བར་དོ་དང
    ལས་ཀྱིས་མ་བུ་མཚམས་སྦྱར་ནས`. Adzom reading: `ལས་ཀྱིས་མ་བུ་སྲིད་པའི་བར་དོ་དང་མཚམས་སྦྱར་ནས`. The fixed sequence repeats ལས་ཀྱིས་མ་བུ in S1190 and S1191. Direct inspection finds one visible occurrence, followed across the row break by བར་དོ་དང and མཚམས་སྦྱར་ནས (layer allocation unresolved). The visible extra fragment སྲིད་པའི (“of existence/becoming,” provisional) stands between མ་བུ and བར་དོ. Exact root-versus-explanation allocation and the origin of the electronic repetition remain unresolved; neither object is certified agreement. English consequence: Retain both fixed lines in the English, with the repeated phrase and uncertain attachment explicitly explained. The print may instead give one “through action, mother and child” construction and a commentarial joining/connection phrase; that possibility does not authorize deleting or rejoining the fixed lines. Evidence: [Adzom p. 489, image 499](https://github.com/Lotus-King-Translation/Mu-Tig-Phreng-Ba/blob/main/evidence/golden/06/00499.png); Row1right–row2left, from ལས་ཀྱིས་མ་བུ through བར་དོ་དང and མཚམས་སྦྱར་ནས (layer allocation unresolved).
